You
You are helping me write a specification using Spec-Driven Development (SDD). This session is ONLY for creating SPEC.md. Do NOT write code, install anything or create other files. Create exactly one file: SPEC.md. ## Project Bunk-o-Meter: a mobile-first web app for Indian college students that tracks class attendance and tells you exactly how many classes you can still skip in each course while staying above the required percentage. Course project; must be deployed so the professor can open it; built mostly autonomously by Claude Code in ~1-2 days. ## Stack (fixed) - Next.js (App Router, TypeScript), Tailwind, Vitest, Playwright for end-to-end tests, deployed on Vercel (Hobby). - Neon Postgres (free), accessed ONLY from server code via DATABASE_URL; the browser never talks to the database. No AI APIs. - Auth.js (NextAuth) with the Google provider. - Env var names: DATABASE_URL, AUTH_SECRET, AUTH_GOOGLE_ID, AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET. Secrets only in .env.local (gitignored) and Vercel; never ask me to paste a key into chat; never read .env.local. - GitHub Actions CI on ubuntu-24.04 (lint, typecheck, unit tests). ## College preset (default rules; all editable per course) - Required attendance: 80% per course by default (some courses differ). - Lectures and tutorials count together per course; a per-course toggle says whether tutorials count at all. - Counting is per class session. - "Strictly below" rule: attended/held >= required passes; anything below, even 79.99% against 80%, fails. No rounding up. - Medical leave counts as PRESENT (tagged "medical"); optional per-course maximum, off by default. - Evaluation window per course, one of: (a) whole semester (checked at endsem), or (b) split: a midsem window (semester start -> midsem date) and an endsem window (midsem date -> semester end), each of which must separately meet the requirement. Budgets are computed per window, and the current window is shown first. - Labs have no attendance percentage. Instead every scheduled lab must be completed: a missed lab becomes a pending makeup lab that can be given a makeup date and marked completed. Show pending makeups prominently. ## Core features 1. Sign in with Google to keep each user's semesters private and synced across devices. Store only name, email and Google ID. /demo works WITHOUT signing in. Automated tests use a test-only credentials sign-in that cannot be enabled in production. A user can delete their account and all data. 2. Semester setup: courses (name, code, colour, rules from the preset), weekly timetable slots (day, start time, duration, type lecture/ tutorial/lab), semester start, midsem date and end date, holidays and no-class periods (exams). 3. Today screen: today's classes with one-tap Present / Absent / Medical / Cancelled (cancelled classes don't count). Extra classes can be added for any date. 4. Bunk budget per course and per window, with exact maths: classes held, attended (incl. medical), current %, remaining scheduled classes in the window, "you can skip N more" or "you must attend the next N", and the % if you attend everything left. Colour-coded safe / warning / danger. 5. Trip planner: choose a date range and see, per course, classes missed, resulting % in the affected window(s), whether still safe, and labs that would need makeups; plus an overall verdict. 6. Bulk marking: mark a date range absent or medical in one go. 7. History calendar with editing of any past day; stats per course (trend over time), most-skipped weekday, streaks; CSV export. 8. Shared class timetable: publish your courses + timetable + dates as a read-only template link; classmates import it as their own semester (a copy) and track their own attendance. 9. Demo mode at /demo (no login): a realistic IISc-style semester with split-window and whole-semester courses, one course in danger, one very safe, and a pending makeup lab. 10. Design: attractive modern dark theme by default plus light theme (Dark/Light/System toggle), mobile-first app feel (bottom navigation, large tap targets, works at 360 px), installable PWA, WCAG AA contrast. ## What SPEC.md must contain (in this order) Goal; users and main scenarios; constraints; inputs/outputs (data model, and the exact attendance formulas with worked examples and edge cases: no classes held yet, required % = 100%, cancelled and extra classes, medical, tutorials excluded, split windows including a class exactly on the midsem date, semester already ended, makeup labs); requirements R1.. in EARS form ("WHEN ... THE SYSTEM SHALL ...", "IF ... THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL ...") each tagged Must or Should; acceptance criteria AC-1.. each objectively pass/fail, mapped to R#, tagged Unit / E2E (Playwright) / Deploy / Manual, preferring Unit and E2E so Claude can verify almost everything itself; Phase-1 deployment risks (Google sign-in on the production domain, Neon from Vercel) explicitly listed; output files; non-goals (push notifications, native apps, AI, syncing with the college's official system); risks with fallbacks; open questions each with a proposed default; changelog (v0.1, today, "Initial draft"). Keep it precise and buildable in 1-2 days: at most ~50 requirements and ~45 ACs. When done, show me a short summary and the open questions.

Written for: Claude Code as the builder, with you and your professor as reviewers. I've written SPEC.md with all twelve sections in the order you asked. It is the only file I created, and I haven't committed it. Summary - Size: 50 requirements in EARS form (44 Must, 6 Should) and 45 acceptance criteria: 17 Unit, 23 E2E, 3 Deploy, 2 Manual. Only the two Manual checks (real Google sign-in across two devices, and installing the app on Android) need a person. - Core maths: pass/fail is the integer test 100·A ≥ p·H. Skips are S = min(R, floor(K/100)), where K = 100(A+R) − p(H+R). "Attend the next N" is N = ceil((pH − 100A)/(100 − p)). Displayed percentages are cut off, not rounded, so 79.96% shows as 79.9%. - 13 worked examples give exact expected outputs. - A brute-force unit test checks every small case against the definition. - Edge cases: all the ones you listed are written out with numbers, plus a few more: past classes left unmarked, absences marked ahead of time, 0% required, a holiday added after marking, a lab-only course, and the midnight boundary in IST. - Fixed demo date: the demo always shows Wed 21 Oct 2026, so it looks the same whenever it's opened. - Test-only extras: I added a non-secret E2E_TEST_AUTH flag. It turns on the test sign-in and an e2e_today cookie that fixes "today" during tests. Both are off whenever the app runs on Vercel. - Build phases: Phase 1 deploys a bare skeleton first, so the Google sign-in and Neon risks show up on day one. The spec lists 12 deployment risks and a short setup checklist for you: Google OAuth client, Neon main and dev branches, Vercel environment variables. - Two behaviours to check: - Cancelling a future class can lower your skip budget. - With a 100% requirement, a course always shows red while classes remain, because there's no slack. Open questions. Each has a default I'll use unless you say otherwise.

● Write(README.md)
⎿  Added 47 lines
     1  # Bunk-o-Meter
     2 +                                                               
     3  Know exactly how many classes you can skip.
     4 +                                                               
     5 +A mobile-first web app for Indian college students. Mark each c
       +lass Present / Absent / Medical / Cancelled, and see, per cours
       +e and per evaluation window, exactly how many more classes you 
       +can skip while staying at or above the required percentage. It 
       +follows strict college rules: 79.99% is below 80%, medical coun
       +ts as present, split midsem/endsem windows, and mandatory labs 
       +with makeups.                                                  
     6 +                                                               
     7 +- **Try it without signing in:** `/demo`                       
     8 +- **Spec:** [SPEC.md](SPEC.md) · **Build plan and AC status:** 
       +[PLAN.md](PLAN.md)                                             
     9 +- **Production URL:** _set after the first Vercel deploy_      
    10 +                                                               
    11 +## Stack                                                       
    12 +                                                               
    13 +Next.js (App Router, TypeScript) · Tailwind CSS · Auth.js (Goog
       +le) · Neon Postgres via Drizzle ORM · Vitest · Playwright · Ver
       +cel · GitHub Actions.                                          
    14 +                                                               
    15 +## Local setup                                                 
    16 +                                                               
    17 +1. Node.js 24 (`.nvmrc`) and npm.                              
    18 +2. `npm ci`                                                    
    19 +3. Create `.env.local` (gitignored) from `.env.example`:       
    20 +   - `DATABASE_URL`: the **dev** branch pooled connection strin
       +g from Neon. Production uses `main`; local, preview and E2E use
       + `dev`.                                                        
    21 +   - `AUTH_SECRET`: any random string, e.g. `openssl rand -base
       +64 32`.                                                        
    22 +   - `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET`: from the Google OA
       +uth client (needed only for real Google sign-in).              
    23 +4. `npm run db:migrate` applies the SQL migrations in `drizzle/
       +` to the dev branch.                                           
    24 +5. `npm run dev` and open http://localhost:3000.               
    25 +                                                               
    26 +## Commands                                                    
    27 +                                                               
    28 +| Command | What it does |                                     
    29 +|---|---|                                                      
    30 +| `npm run check` | Lint, typecheck and unit tests (what CI run
       +s) |                                                           
    31 +| `npm test` | Vitest unit tests (`tests/unit/`) |             
    32 +| `npm run test:e2e` | Playwright: builds and starts the app on
       + port 3100 with test-only sign-in, against the dev database |  
    33 +| `npm run db:generate` | Create a migration from `src/lib/db/s
       +chema.ts` |                                                    
    34 +| `npm run db:migrate` | Apply migrations to `DATABASE_URL` |  
    35 +| `npm run build` | Production build. On Vercel, `vercel-build`
       + runs migrations first. |                                      
    36 +                                                               
    37 +## Deploying                                                   
    38 +                                                               
    39 +Vercel builds on every push to `main`. Environment variables (s
       +et in Vercel, never committed):                                
    40 +                                                               
    41 +| Name | Production | Preview |                                
    42 +|---|---|---|                                                  
    43 +| `DATABASE_URL` | Neon `main` pooled URL | Neon `dev` pooled U
       +RL |                                                           
    44 +| `AUTH_SECRET` | random secret | random secret |              
    45 +| `AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`, `AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET` | Google OAuth client 
       +| same |                                                       
    46 +                                                               
    47 +Google OAuth redirect URIs: `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/cal
       +lback/google` and `https://<production-domain>/api/auth/callbac
       +k/google`.                                                     
    48 +                                                               
    49 +`E2E_TEST_AUTH` must never be set on Vercel; test-only sign-in 
       +is also disabled whenever `VERCEL` is set.

        -ployments and E2E use `dev`.

     41 +The college preset applies to every new course, and every rule
        + can be edited per course:                                    
     42  
     43 -## Testing                                                    
     43 +- **Required %:** 80% by default (any whole number from 0 to 1
        +00).                                                          
     44 +- **Counting:** per class session. A 3-hour lab counts as one 
        +session.                                                      
     45 +- **What counts:** lectures always. Tutorials only if "tutoria
        +ls count" is on. Labs never count toward the percentage.      
     46 +- **Cancelled classes** don't count at all. **Extra classes** 
        +count like any other.                                         
     47 +- **Medical** counts as attended. An optional cap per course l
        +imits how many medical sessions count; later ones count as abs
        +ent. The earliest medical sessions in the semester count first
        +.                                                             
     48 +- **Evaluation window:** the whole semester, or split into a *
        +*midsem** window (start → midsem date, inclusive) and an **end
        +sem** window (the day after midsem → end). Each split window m
        +ust meet the requirement on its own.                          
     49 +- **Labs:** every missed lab (absent or medical) becomes a **p
        +ending makeup** until it is marked completed. A makeup is **ov
        +erdue** once its makeup date has passed.                      
     50 +- **Unmarked past classes** don't count either way; the app as
        +ks you to mark them. Absences you mark ahead of time count str
        +aight away.                                                   
     51  
     45 -| Command | What it does |                                    
     52 +For each course and window, with required % `p`:              
     53 +                                                              
     54 +- `H` = classes held (marked present, absent or medical)      
     55 +- `A` = attended (present, plus medical within the cap)       
     56 +- `R` = classes still to come (unmarked, from today on)       
     57 +                                                              
     58 +| Quantity | Formula |                                        
     59  |---|---|
     47 -| `npm run check` | Lint, typecheck and unit tests. This is ex
        -actly what CI runs. |                                         
     48 -| `npm test` | Vitest unit tests (`tests/unit/`): worked examp
        -les, a brute-force sweep, edge cases |                        
     49 -| `npm run test:e2e` | Playwright: builds and starts the app o
        -n port 3100 with test-only sign-in, then runs `e2e/` against t
        -he dev database |                                             
     50 -| `npm run db:generate` | Creates a migration from `src/lib/db
        -/schema.ts` |                                                 
     51 -| `npm run db:migrate` | Applies migrations to `DATABASE_URL` 
        -|                                                             
     60 +| Requirement met | `100·A ≥ p·H` (integers; **strictly-below 
        +fails, no rounding**) |                                       
     61 +| Current % | `floor(1000·A/H) / 10`, truncated to one decimal
        + and never rounded up |                                       
     62 +| Slack | `K = 100·(A+R) − p·(H+R)` |                         
     63 +| Skips left | `S = min(R, floor(K/100))` when `K ≥ 0` |      
     64 +| Classes to attend in a row to get back to `p` | `N = ceil((p
        +·H − 100·A) / (100 − p))` |                                   
     65 +| Best % still possible | `floor(1000·(A+R)/(H+R)) / 10` |    
     66  
     53 -How E2E tests sign in: they use a credentials provider that ex
        -ists only when `E2E_TEST_AUTH=1` and `VERCEL` is unset. Playwr
        -ight sets the flag itself. Each test creates its own `<uuid>@e
        -2e.test` user and deletes it afterwards.                      
     67 +**Status** is decided by the first rule that matches. It is al
        +ways shown as text and an icon, never colour alone.           
     68  
     69 +| Order | Condition | Status | Headline |                     
     70 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
     71 +| 1 | No classes in the window | none | — |                   
     72 +| 2 | Nothing left (`R = 0`) | safe or danger | *Final X% · me
        +ets/below p%* |                                               
     73 +| 3 | `K < 0` | danger | *Can't recover in this window · best 
        +X%* |                                                         
     74 +| 4 | `S = 0` | danger | *No skips left* |                    
     75 +| 5 | Below `p` now | warning | *Attend next N* |             
     76 +| 6 | `S ≤ 2` | warning | *Can skip S more* |                 
     77 +| 7 | Otherwise | safe | *Can skip S more* |                  
     78 +                                                              
     79 +Worked example: needs 80%, 17 of 20 attended, 20 classes left.
        + `K = 3700 − 3200 = 500`, so you can skip **5 more**. That end
        +s at 32/40 = exactly 80%, which passes; a 6th skip ends at 77.
        +5%, which fails.                                              
     80 +                                                              
     81 +SPEC.md §4.4 has all 14 worked examples and the edge cases.   
     82 +                                                              
     83 +## Running it locally                                         
     84 +                                                              
     85 +1. Install Node.js 24 (see `.nvmrc`) and npm. Run `npm ci`.   
     86 +2. Create `.env.local` (it is gitignored) from `.env.example`:
     87 +   - `DATABASE_URL`: the Neon **dev** branch pooled URL. Produ

    130 +## Known limitations                                          
    131  
     71 -Never set `E2E_TEST_AUTH` on Vercel. `GET /api/health` returns
        - `{"ok":true,"db":true}` when the database answers.           
    132 +- **No Google sign-in on preview deployments.** Vercel preview
        + URLs change on every deploy and Google doesn't allow wildcard
        + redirect URIs. Use production, localhost or `/demo`.         
    133 +- **Offline is read-only.** The installed app opens offline fr
        +om its cache, but marking needs a connection. There is no offl
        +ine queue.                                                    
    134 +- **India time only.** "Today" is always the Asia/Kolkata date
        +.                                                             
    135 +- **Required % must be a whole number** (e.g. 75, 80). Courses
        + with different rules for lectures and tutorials aren't modell
        +ed; the only option is whether tutorials count at all.        
    136 +- **Absences marked ahead of time count immediately**, so a pl
        +anned trip lowers your current % now. The trip planner is the 
        +what-if tool; it saves nothing.                               
    137 +- **Share links are live.** A link shows the owner's current t
        +imetable. Each import is a frozen copy and doesn't receive lat
        +er changes.                                                   
    138 +- **Free-tier hosting.** The Neon database sleeps when idle, s
        +o the first request after a pause can take a second or two. Th
        +e database is in AWS US East, so pages are slower from India t
        +han a regional database would give.                           
    139 +- **E2E tests are not in CI.** They need a database, so they r
        +un locally against the Neon `dev` branch. CI runs lint, typech
        +eck and unit tests.                                           
    140 +- **The demo date is fixed** at 21 Oct 2026 on purpose.       
    141 +- **Out of scope:** notifications, native apps, AI features, a
        +nd syncing with a college's official attendance system.       
    142 +

    144 +                                                              
    145 +This project was built with Spec-Driven Development: requireme
        +nts → acceptance criteria → tests → code.                     
    146 +                                                              
    147 +| File | Role |                                               
    148 +|---|---|                                                     
    149 +| [SPEC.md](SPEC.md) | The specification: goal, users, constra
        +ints, data model, exact formulas with worked examples, 51 EARS
        + requirements (R1–R51), 47 acceptance criteria (AC-1–AC-47), d
        +eployment risks, decisions, changelog |                       
    150 +| [PLAN.md](PLAN.md) | The build plan: 12 phases with [AUTO]/[
        +HUMAN] tasks, the rules followed during the autonomous build, 
        +the AC status and evidence table, and build notes |           
    151 +| `tests/unit/`, `e2e/` | One test file per AC, named in PLAN.
        +md's AC table |                                               
    152 +| Git history | One commit per phase, each referencing the `R#
        +` / `AC-#` IDs it delivers |

⎿  Updated README.md (+152 -152)
      1 -# Bunk-o-Meter                                                
      2 -                                                              
      3 -**Know exactly how many classes you can skip.**               
      4 -                                                              
      5 -- **Live app:** https://bunkometer.vercel.app                 
      6 -- **For evaluators, with no sign-in needed:** https://bunkomet
        -er.vercel.app/demo                                            
      7 -                                                              
      8 -Bunk-o-Meter is a mobile-first web app for Indian college stud
        -ents. You mark each class with one tap: Present, Absent, Medic
        -al or Cancelled. For every course and every evaluation window,
        - it then tells you exactly how many more classes you can skip 
        -and still stay at or above the required attendance. The maths 
        -is exact integer arithmetic, so 79.99% never rounds up to 80%.
      9 -                                                              
     10 -## The demo (`/demo`)                                         
     11 -                                                              
     12 -The demo is a realistic IISc-style Aug–Dec 2026 semester, froz
        -en at **Wed 21 Oct 2026** so it looks the same whenever it's o
        -pened. It shows every feature:                                
     13 -                                                              
     14 -| Course | What it shows |                                    
     15 -|---|---|                                                     
     16 -| PH 203 | **In danger** in its endsem window, even though its
        - midsem window passed (windows are independent) |             
     17 -| MA 211 | Very safe (split windows, tutorials count) |       
     18 -| CH 201 | Recovering: tutorials not counted, and medical leav
        -e within a cap |
